<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, December 8, 2015 
PRESENT: Robert E. Black Jr., Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse Thompson, Supervisor; Jason E. 
Whiting, Vice-Chairman; and Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman were present.  
 
9:04 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation was led by Supervisor Thompson.  
      CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Terry Hill, Commander of VFW Post 9907 in Show Low, addressed the Board about 
their accomplishments in the community . He indicated that 261 people were served at the Veterans  Day 
Appreciation D inner. He reported they also  hosted a Thanksgiving luncheon and sponsored a Family Day  
luncheon for the National Guard Unit  and their families in the Show Low area.  He indicated that in December, 
the VFW will provide  for needy families food baskets and gifts for 53 families , with 64 children. Commander 
Hill stated that the VFW is in support of Brad Carlyon, County Attorney  and the newly established Veterans 
Court. Kevin Smith of  7144 Hwy 260 Show Low, addressed the Board regarding an order of protection served 
by Constable Scott Tipton on his friend Ralph Cunningham. Mr. Smith said the action taken by Constable Tipton 
was not appropriate. Karen Smith of 7144 Hwy 260 Show Low, reported the incident involving Constable Scott 
Tipton and his actions on her property were done without any warrant, order or paperwork provided. Mr. Ralph 
Cunningham of 2144 State Rt. 260  Show Low, addressed the Board regarding his arrest , and his vehicle and 
property taken by Constable Scott Tipton.   
  
      CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: November 19, 2015 Joint Study Session and November 24, 2015 b) Warrant 
& Voucher Report over $1000 November 2015 c) Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, October-November 
2015 d) 2016 Board of Supervisors Meeting Schedule e) E -Co Certifying Officer Designation for the 
Environmental Review requirements of ADOH CDBG funds f) Approval of the two year term for Robinson 
Honani to serve on the Navajo County Extension Advisory Board to expire on April 1, 2016 and a three year term 
for Allison Hephne r to serve on the Navajo County Extension Advisory Board to expire on April 1, 2017 g) 
Approval of IGA between the Sheriff’s Office and Cities of Winslow and Show Low, Towns of Snowflake and 
Taylor and Pinetop-Lakeside regarding Byrne grant funding for FY 16 h) Thank you letters to Governor Doug 
Ducey, Senator John McCain, Senator Jeff Flake, and Congresswoman Ann Kirkpatrick regarding the Apache 
Railway i) Sympathy letter to Kameron Edwards and family: Supervisor Williams made a motion to authorize 
the Chairwoman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion. 
HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Eric Scott reviewed the 26 
item personnel action report. Supervisor Thompson made a motion to approve the Personnel Action Report as 
presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS : Introduction of the 2016 Miss Navajo County & Miss Navajo County’s 
Outstanding Teen : Emily Eavenson address ed the Board regarding her platform as the 2015 Miss Navajo 
County’s Outstanding Teen. Samantha Edwards, Miss Navajo County 2016 reported on  her platform regarding 
Domestic Violence. The Board members thanked both ladies for their leadership roles in the communities. 
 
      WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY R ECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT: Board  of 
Directors Session: Supervisor Thompson made a motion to enter into the White Mountain Lakes County

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 12-08-2015 
    Page 2 of 4 
 
 
 
Recreation Improvement District Board of Directors Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion.  Consideration and possible approval of an amendment to the existing White 
Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District Advisory Board By-Laws: Jerry McGraw, newly 
elected Chairman of the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District Advisory Board, 
discussed his appointment as Chairman and duties that come  with Chairman o f the advisory board. He also  
discussed the recommendation of an amendment to the advisory board’s by-laws. Supervisor Whiting expressed 
appreciation to Chairman McGraw for his service to  the community as the Chief of White Mountain Lake Fire 
District and to the advisory board. Supervisor Whiting indicated that he supports the recommendation that was 
made. Chairwoman Whitesinger opened the Call to the Public . Marc Silliman  of 1816 Howard Place White 
Mountain Lake, stated he would like to speak  regarding what he felt was a malfeasance in office by the newly 
elected Chairman of the advisory committee. He reported that newspaper articles have been written about the way 
the advisory board has made changes to the by -laws in their meetings. Mr. Silliman stated that he believes that 
Mr. McGraw wanted to remove Norma Loeffler from the advisor y committee, but leave her as an employee 
working as lake patrol. He indicated he felt Mr. McGraw was incorrect in his actions of not considering Ms. 
Loeffler’s vote in the meeting . Mr. Silliman stated that  the advisory board has n o authority to take action to 
remove members in accordance to the by-laws which were written by Attorney Brandt Clark and adopted by the 
Board of Supervisors, acting as the Board of Directors. Mr. Silliman asked the Board of Supervisors if they would 
postpone the adoption of the proposed by-law changes today, to allow the advisory committee time to select 
additional members who can convene and ratify the proposed change and consider other changes to the by-laws 
regarding the procedures for financial expenditures, which was addressed by Mr. McGraw. Mr. Silliman also 
asked the Board to consider and answer the question of Norma Loffler’s employment or removal from the 
committee with the district, and to consider the issue of malfeasance in office by Mr. McGraw.  
      At 9:45 a.m. Supervisor Thompson left the meeting. 
      John Misener of 1827 Gutierrez Place White Mountain Lake, addressed the Board and stated he approved of the 
way the lake has been ran for the last ten years. He stated a small group of people were not happy with the rules. 
Mr. Misener indicated he would like to see that the advisory committee be fully staffed with 7 members to make 
decisions, before the Board of Supervisors makes their  decision. Lyn Misener  of 1827 Gutierrez Place White 
Mountain Lake, addressed the B oard about the concern s of the community and the way the changes are being 
made. She indicated she enjoys the lake and stated that it is being run well, with only a few that do not like the 
rules. Supervisor Whiting asked if the committee voted to recommend this change to the by-laws. Jerry McGraw 
indicated that there are four committee members  and that the vote was 3 -1. He indicated that Brandt Clark 
informed them to proceed with that number of com mittee members. Supervisor Whiting inquired if  Norma 
Loeffler was asked to abstain from the vote and discussion held about her position on the advisory board. Norma 
Loeffler stated that that she was an asset to the committee and votes in good faith. Supervisor Whiting indicated 
that he felt that employees should attend the meetings, to offer data, support and to make recommendations to the 
committee, however he felt that paid employees should not be able to vote. Mr. McGraw indicated that things 
have continued to run, by the bylaws as it has had in the past, and vacant members have been seated without the 
Board of Supervisors approval. Supervisor Whiting indicated that he as well as other staff members have spent 
a lot of time on the issues of this committee . He stated that together, we need to find a way to move forward to 
benefit the community. Marc Silliman addressed the Board again, asking that this item be postponed and further 
asked for a point of order. Brad Carlyon , County Attorney  indicated that Norma  Loeffler will have to make a 
decision if she wants to be an employee or a committee member. Supervisor Whiting  asked if the Board’s 
decision should wait until the White Mountain Lakes Advisory Board has a 7 member committee. Chief McGraw 
indicated that he would not have an issue with it. Supervisor Whiting made a motion to table this item until the 
White Mountain Lakes Advisory Board could  meet and make a recommendation to the Board of Supervisors for 
their decision, to be put on the January 26th Board of Supervisor’s agenda or a Board meeting the first of February.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 12-08-2015 
    Page 3 of 4 
 
 
 
Mr. McGraw indicated that new Board members will be seated at their December 16 meeting, then at the January 
12th Board of Supervisors meeting the new recommended advisory board members will be approved. Mr. McGraw 
stated that the committee members can have a special meeting before the Board of Directors meeting on January 
26th or February 9, to approve the new proposed by -law changes. Supervisor Whiting made a motion  to table 
the consideration and possible a pproval of an amendment to the existing White Mountain Lakes County 
Recreation Improvement District Advisory Board By -Laws to a futur e date; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion . Supervisor Williams made a motion  to return to regular 
session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
ADMINISTRATION:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of an Intergovernmental Agreement for the creation of Northeast 
Arizona Regional Dispatch Center (NARDC) : Paul Watson , Assistant County Manager  presented the 
item. Mr. Watson indicated  this Intergovernmental Agreement  has come together through the diligent 
work of many  partnerships of the Mayors and City Councils, incl uding Holbrook, Taylor, Snowflake 
Pinetop-Lakeside, Show Low, the City Chiefs of Police, the many Fire Departments, Sheriff K.C. Clark, 
Attorney Jason Moore and Attorney Morgan Brown . Mr. Watson stated  that this IGA will improve 
dispatch services in Northe astern Arizona and minimize response time for law enforcement, fire and 
emergency medical services, thus saving lives and providing better public service. Brad Carlyon, County 
Attorney indicated that there will be a cost to the county for capi tal costs, ho wever there are many 
beneficial reasons to do this . He indicated it will save  money in the future and reported on  the new 
computer functions tha t will make dispatch services  more efficient by combining information into one 
database. James Jayne indicated that the creation of a consolidated emergency services communication 
center serves the public interests. Mr. Jayne spoke about the partnerships and the time spent in moving 
the agreement forward. He thanked Jason Moore, James Menlove, Homero Vela and Paul Watson for their 
efforts. Supervisor Whiting made a motion to approve the Intergovernmental Agreement for the creation 
of Northeast Arizona Regional Dispatch Center (NARDC) ; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. 
Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Presentation of the Apache Railway : Paul Watson gave a presentation of the Apache Railway  and its 
history, which has been vital in supporting economic development in Navajo County. He spoke about the 
three year effort to save and secure the Apache Railway through a collaborative local and state effort. Mr. 
Watson and Supervisor Whiting expressed their appreciation to the Navajo County staff ; James Jayne, 
Homero Vela, James Menlove, Brad Carlyon and Jason Moore for their leadership. They spoke about the 
partnerships and efforts to strengthen our economy, in finding equity investors to contribute capital to the 
acquisition of the railway which included Governor Doug Ducey, Senator Jeff Flake, Senator John 
McCain, Congresswoman Ann Kirkpatrick, Steve Brophy, Presid ent of Aztec Land & Cattle, Attorney 
David Brown, Arizona Commerce Authority , Town of Snowflake  and staff , Arizona MultiBank and 
Clearinghouse CDFI, and the Apache Railway employees . Supervisor Whiting presented Paul Watson 
with a plaque. James Jayne and Paul Watso n recognized Supervisor Whiting with a framed letter from 
the Apache Railway.  
      COUNTY ATTORNEY: Consideration and possible approval of Child Support Enforcement Intergovernmental 
Agreement between Arizona Department of Security and Navajo County Attorney’s Office : Lynda Young 
discussed the item. Supervisor Black made a motion  to approve the Child Support Enforcement 
Intergovernmental Agreement between Arizona Department of Security and Na vajo County Attorney’s Office ; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 12-08-2015 
    Page 4 of 4 
 
 
 
      COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Chairwoman Whitesinger introduced her family. She thanked everyone that helped make this year successful  
and expressed gratitude to the staff for their support. She indicated Mary  Springer went to Cibecue Elementary 
School regarding sheltering for emergency management. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Homero Vela for his 
leadership and service. Supervisor Black gave an update on chapter meetings in District I. He reported on an 
emergency management meeting in Kayenta and Pinon. Supervisor Black  thanked Homero Vela for his 
assistance and wished everyone a Merry Christmas. Supervisor Williams thanked Chairwoman Whitesinger 
for her service as Chairwoman. Supervisor Whiting expressed his gratitude to Chairwoman Whitesinger and 
thanked everyone who helped with the Holbrook parade and wished everyone  a Merry Christmas. Mr. Jayne 
thanked Lynda Young, Ricky Denton and  those that hel ped with the Show Low parade. Mr. Jayne expressed 
thanks to Chairwoman Whitesinger and her family for her service  as Chairwoman. James Menlove, Finance 
Director thanked Paige Peterson and Olivia Todd for t heir work on the budget book for Fiscal Year 2015/2016. 
Mr. Menlove introduced Daniel Duchon, Management Analysist in the finance department.  
      ADJOURN: At 11:17 a.m. Supervisor Williams made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
 
APPROVED:       DATED: January 12, 2016 
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
